A Motivic Snaith Decomposition
Viktor Kleen

Abstract
Abstract The filtration BGL 0 ⊂ … ⊂ BGL n − 1 ⊂ BGL n \mathord{\textnormal{BGL}}_{0}\mathchoice{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\displaystyle\subset$}}}{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\textstyle\subset$}}}{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\subset$}}}{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\subset$}}}\dots\mathchoice{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\displaystyle\subset$}}}{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\textstyle\subset$}}}{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\subset$}}}{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\subset$}}}\mathord{\textnormal{BGL}}_{n-1}\mathchoice{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\displaystyle\subset$}}}{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\textstyle\subset$}}}{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\subset$}}}{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\subset$}}}\mathord{\textnormal{BGL}}_{n} is split by motivic Becker–Gottlieb transfers in the motivic stable homotopy category over any scheme. This recovers results by Snaith on the splitting of BGL n ​ ( ℂ ) \mathord{\textnormal{BGL}}_{n}(\mathbb{C}) in classical stable homotopy theory by passing to complex realizations. On the way, we extend motivic homotopy theory to smooth ind-schemes as bases and show how to construct the necessary fragment of the six operations and duality for this extension.



\RedeclareSectionCommand

[counterwithin=section,font=]paragraph

1
Introduction

Snaith
[
Sna79
,
Sna78
]
described splittings of the natural filtrations on
\mathord{\textnormal{BGL}}_{n}(\mathbb{C})
and
\mathord{\textnormal{BGL}}_{n}(\mathbb{H})
in the classical stable homotopy category, using Becker–Gottlieb transfer associated with the normalizer of a maximal torus in
\mathord{\textnormal{GL}}_{n}
.
He also obtained coarser results for
\mathord{\textnormal{BGL}}_{n}(\mathbb{R})
and for finite fields.
Using transfers associated with the block-diagonal subgroup
\mathord{\textnormal{GL}}_{i}\times\mathord{\textnormal{GL}}_{n-i}\mathchoice{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\displaystyle\subset$}}}{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\textstyle\subset$}}}{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\subset$}}}{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\subset$}}}\mathord{\textnormal{GL}}_{n}
,
[
MP89
]
recovered Snaith’s results on
\mathord{\textnormal{BGL}}_{n}(\mathbb{C})
and
\mathord{\textnormal{BGL}}_{n}(\mathbb{H})
and improve the ones on
\mathord{\textnormal{BGL}}_{n}(\mathbb{R})
and
\mathord{\textnormal{BGL}}_{n}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
.
In fact, the applicability of this approach to the case of
\mathord{\textnormal{BGL}}_{n}(\mathbb{C})
was first noted by Richter.

In this paper, we prove an analogue of Snaith’s, Mitchell and Priddy’s and Richter’s result in the motivic stable homotopy category of an arbitrary base scheme
S
:

Theorem 1.1
.

Over any scheme
S
, there is a
\mathbb{P}^{1}_{S}
–stable splitting
\mathord{\textnormal{BGL}}_{m,+}\simeq\bigvee_{i=1}^{m}\mathord{\textnormal{BGL}}_{i}/\mathord{\textnormal{BGL}}_{i-1}
of the natural filtration
\mathord{\textnormal{BGL}}_{0}\mathchoice{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\displaystyle\subset$}}}{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\textstyle\subset$}}}{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\subset$}}}{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\subset$}}}\dots\mathchoice{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\displaystyle\subset$}}}{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\textstyle\subset$}}}{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\subset$}}}{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\subset$}}}\mathord{\textnormal{BGL}}_{m-1}\mathchoice{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\displaystyle\subset$}}}{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\textstyle\subset$}}}{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\subset$}}}{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\subset$}}}\mathord{\textnormal{BGL}}_{m}
.

Using topological realization, for
S=\Spec(\mathbb{C})
or
S=\Spec(\mathbb{R})
, this recovers the classical story.
Our approach mirrors the technique of
[
MP89
]
, utilizing Becker–Gottlieb transfers associated with the block-diagonal inclusion
\mathord{\textnormal{BGL}}_{i}\times\mathord{\textnormal{BGL}}_{n-i}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hbox to16.4pt{\vbox to5.2pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.23187pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\ignorespaces}}{{\ignorespaces}{}}{{}}{}

.
We make essential use of the six operations approach to the motivic Becker–Gottlieb transfer introduced by
[
Hoy14
]
.
The main technical difficulty in our approach to the splitting of
\mathord{\textnormal{BGL}}_{n,+}
is the fact that
\mathord{\textnormal{BGL}}_{n}
is not representable by a scheme.
However, it is representable by a smooth ind-scheme.

}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.62 L 17.4 2.62}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\ignorespaces}}{{{\ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{12.68755pt}{1.89444pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -1.62 1.87 C -1.32 0.75 -0.66 0.22 0 0 C -0.66 -0.22 -1.32 -0.75 -1.62 -1.87}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\ignorespaces}{\ignorespaces}{\ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}}\mathord{\textnormal{Gr}}_{n}
is then defined to be the image in
\SH(S)
of the monoidal trace of
U
in
\SH(\mathord{\textnormal{Gr}}_{n})
.
We appeal to a result of
[
May01
]
to see that this construction of Becker–Gottlieb transfers has a Mayer–Vietoris property.
This enables us to show that, just like in classical topology, the transfers of the inclusions
\mathord{\textnormal{BGL}}_{i}\times\mathord{\textnormal{BGL}}_{n-i}\mathchoice{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\displaystyle\subset$}}}{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\textstyle\subset$}}}{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\subset$}}}{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\subset$}}}\mathord{\textnormal{BGL}}_{n}
can be used to split the natural filtration on
\mathord{\textnormal{BGL}}_{n}
.
Since the transfer is a stable phenomenon, this splitting is naturally only found in the stable motivic world.

. It is a

\infty
–category in the sense of
[
Lur09
]
. We refer to
\H(X)

interchangably as the
\mathbb{A}^{1}
–homotopy category of
X
or the motivic
homotopy category of
X
. The associated pointed
\infty
–category will be denoted by

\H_{\bullet}(X)
. Inverting
(\mathbb{P}^{1},\infty)\in\H_{\bullet}(X)
with respect to
the smash product yields the stable motivic homotopy category
\SH(X)
of

X
. It is a symmetric monoidal, presentable, stable
\infty
–category in the sense of

[
Lur12
]
. An account of this definition of
\H(X)
,

\H_{\bullet}(X)
and
\SH(X)
for noetherian schemes and its equivalence to
the approach of
[
MV99
]
is given in
[
Rob15
]
, the
generalization to arbitrary schemes can be found in

[
Hoy14
, Appendix C]
.

We follow
[
Lev18
]
in writing
X/S\in\SH(S)
for the

\mathbb{P}^{1}
–suspension spectrum of a smooth scheme
X
over
S
. We will
write
X_{+}\in\H_{\bullet}(S)
for
X
with a disjoint basepoint added. We
sometimes do not distinguish notationally between the pointed motivic space

X_{+}\in\H_{\bullet}(S)
and its
\mathbb{P}^{1}
–suspension spectrum
X/S=X_{+}\in\SH(S)
.

When dealing with ind-schemes we have elected not to speak of

“ind-smooth”
schemes and morphisms. Instead, for us a smooth morphisms
between ind-schemes will be what is usually called an ind-smooth morphism,
namely a formal colimit of smooth morphisms.

are identities. In this situation
{X}^{\vee}
is said to be a
right dual

of
X
and
X
is said to be a
left dual
of
{X}^{\vee}
. If
X

is additionally a right dual of
{X}^{\vee}
, then
X
is said to be

strongly dualizable
with dual
{X}^{\vee}
.

A duality datum in a symmetric monoidal
\infty
–category
\mathcal{C}
is a duality datum
in the homotopy category
\textnormal{h}\mathcal{C}
, see

[
Lur12
, section 4.6.1]
.

Remark 2.2
.

In
[
Lev18
]
, Levine defines a dual
{X}^{\vee}=\map(X,\mathbf{1})
for any object
X
in a
closed
symmetric monoidal
category. Then
X
is called strongly dualizable whenever the induced morphism

{X}^{\vee}\mathchoice{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\displaystyle\otimes$}}}{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\textstyle\otimes$}}}{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\otimes$}}}{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\otimes$}}}X\mathrel{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hbox to16.4pt{\vbox to5.2pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.23187pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\ignorespaces}}{{\ignorespaces}{}}{{}}{}
{\ignorespaces}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \ignorespaces{}{{
{
\lxSVG@setdash{0.0pt}\ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundcap\ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\ignorespaces{}
{\ignorespaces}{}{}
{}{}{}
\lxSVG@stroke}}

Proposition 2.5
.

(i)

Y

U
and
V
are smooth ind-schemes over a smooth ind-scheme
B
over

S
. Further suppose that there is a homotopy cocartesian square

in
\SH(B)
. Assume that

,
U/B
and
V/B
are strongly

\lxSVG@setdash{0.0pt}\ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundcap\ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\ignorespaces{}
{\ignorespaces}{}{}
{}{}{}
\lxSVG@stroke}}
}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.62 L 17.4 2.62}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\ignorespaces}}{{{\ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{12.68755pt}{1.89444pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -1.62 1.87 C -1.32 0.75 -0.66 0.22 0 0 C -0.66 -0.22 -1.32 -0.75 -1.62 -1.87}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\ignorespaces}{\ignorespaces}{\ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}}B
are maps of smooth ind-schemes over
S
and

E/B
is strongly dualizable in
\SH(B)
then the pullback

p^{*}(E/B)\simeq(E\times_{B}B^{\prime})/B^{\prime}
is strongly dualizable in
\SH(B^{\prime})

and
\operatorname{Tr}(p^{*}f\,/B^{\prime})\simeq p^{*}\operatorname{Tr}(f/B)
.

(iii)

The absolute transfer is natural in cartesian squares: If

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

is a cartesian square of smooth ind-schemes over
S
and the vertical maps are
smooth, then the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E^{\prime}/S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E/S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B^{\prime}/S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B/S}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Tr}(f^{\prime}/S)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Tr}(f/S)}

commutes in
\SH(S)
.

To prove part (i) of
Proposition 2.5
we appeal to a general
additivity result of May’s. In the context of symmetric monoidal triangulated
categories,
[
May01
]
proves that the transfer is additive in
distinguished triangles. However, since duality in symmetric monoidal
\infty
–categories is characterised at the level of homotopy categories, May’s theorem admits the
following reformulation.

(
[
May01
, Theorem 1.9]
)
.

}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.62 L 17.4 2.62}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\ignorespaces}}{{{\ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{12.68755pt}{1.89444pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -1.62 1.87 C -1.32 0.75 -0.66 0.22 0 0 C -0.66 -0.22 -1.32 -0.75 -1.62 -1.87}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\ignorespaces}{\ignorespaces}{\ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}}Y\mathchoice{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\displaystyle\otimes$}}}{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\textstyle\otimes$}}}{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\otimes$}}}{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\otimes$}}}C
and that
X
and
Y
are
strongly dualizable. Then
Z
is strongly dualizable and there are maps

\Delta_{X}
and
\Delta_{Z}
such that

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\mathchoice{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\displaystyle\otimes$}}}{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\textstyle\otimes$}}}{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\otimes$}}}{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\otimes$}}}C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y\mathchoice{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\displaystyle\otimes$}}}{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\textstyle\otimes$}}}{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\otimes$}}}{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\otimes$}}}C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z\mathchoice{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\displaystyle\otimes$}}}{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\textstyle\otimes$}}}{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\otimes$}}}{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\otimes$}}}C}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{Y}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{Z}}

commutes. Furthermore, we have
\operatorname{tr}_{Y,\Delta_{Y}}=\operatorname{tr}_{X,\Delta_{X}}+\operatorname{tr}_{Z,\Delta_{Z}}
in
\pi_{0}\map_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathbf{1}_{,}C)
.

Part (ii) of
Proposition 2.5
is proven in

[
Lev18
, Lemma 1.6]
. We formulate the proof of part (iii) as
a lemma.

is smooth with
E/B\in\SH(B)
strongly
dualizable and

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{\prime}}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}

is cartesian. Then the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E^{\prime}/S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E/S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B^{\prime}/S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B/S}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Tr}(f^{\prime}/S)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Tr}(f/S)}

is homotopy commutative.

[
MV99
, Proposition 4.3.7]
shows that the colimit
U_{r}(\infty)=\colim_{N}U_{r}(N)
along these embeddings is contractible in
\H(S)

quotient

\operatorname{\textnormal{Gr}}_{r}(N)
and consequently

is a model for
\mathord{\textnormal{BGL}}_{r}
.

∎

Lemma 3.3
.

The morphism
\overline{i_{r,n}}
is a Zariski–locally trivial bundle over

\mathord{\textnormal{Gr}}_{n}
. Its fiber is the quotient
\mathord{\textnormal{GL}}_{n}/(\mathord{\textnormal{GL}}_{r}\times\mathord{\textnormal{GL}}_{n-r})
.

}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.62 L 17.4 2.62}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\ignorespaces}}{{{\ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{12.68755pt}{1.89444pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -1.62 1.87 C -1.32 0.75 -0.66 0.22 0 0 C -0.66 -0.22 -1.32 -0.75 -1.62 -1.87}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\ignorespaces}{\ignorespaces}{\ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}}\mathord{\textnormal{Gr}}_{n}
defines a dualizable object in
\SH(\operatorname{\textnormal{Gr}}_{n}(N))
for
all
N
. But, by
Lemma 3.3
the morphism
i
is a
Zariski-locally trivial fiber bundle over
\operatorname{\textnormal{Gr}}_{n}(N)
with fiber

X=\mathord{\textnormal{GL}}_{n}/(\mathord{\textnormal{GL}}_{r}\times\mathord{\textnormal{GL}}_{n-r})
. Hence, to show that
i
defines a strongly
dualizable object in
\SH(\operatorname{\textnormal{Gr}}_{n}(N))
, by
Theorem 2.9

it is enough to show that
X/S\in\SH(S)
is strongly dualizable.

But we have seen that
X\simeq\operatorname{\textnormal{Gr}}_{r}(n)
in
\H(S)
and therefore also in

\SH(S)
. The scheme
\operatorname{\textnormal{Gr}}_{r}(n)
is smooth and proper over
S
, so
motivic Atiyah duality,
Theorem 2.8
, implies that

\operatorname{\textnormal{Gr}}_{r}(n)/S
and therefore also
X/S
is strongly dualizable in

\SH(S)
, see for example
[
Lev18
, Proposition 1.2]
.
∎

. It follows that the complement

of the last lemma yields a

–torsor

over

\mathord{\textnormal{Gr}}_{n-1}
and obtain a homotopy cocartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(U_{n-1}(\infty)\times^{\mathord{\textnormal{GL}}_{n-1}}(U\cap V))/\mathord{\textnormal{Gr}}_{n-1}}

\Autoref
thm:transfer-additivity then shows that

\operatorname{tr}_{E/\mathord{\textnormal{Gr}}_{n-1}}=\operatorname{tr}_{G_{r,n-1}}+\operatorname{tr}_{G_{r-1,n-1}}-\operatorname{tr}_{X/\mathord{\textnormal{Gr}}_{n-1}}

in
\SH(\mathord{\textnormal{Gr}}_{n-1})
. Passing to the absolute transfer and using

Lemma 2.7
yields that
\varphi
is the sum of

4
Proof of the Theorem

∎

Appendix A
Stable Motivic Homotopy Theory of Smooth Ind-Schemes

We freely use the theory of presentable
\infty
–categories as developed in

[
Lur09
, section 5.5.3]
. The
\infty
–category of presentable
\infty
–categories with
left adjoints as morphisms is denoted
\mathcal{P\mkern-4.0mur}^{\textnormal{L}}
while the
\infty
–category of presentable

\infty
–categories with right adjoints as morphisms is denoted
\mathcal{P\mkern-4.0mur}^{\textnormal{R}}
. There is an
equivalence
\mathcal{P\mkern-4.0mur}^{\textnormal{L}}\simeq(\mathcal{P\mkern-4.0mur}^{\textnormal{R}})^{\textnormal{op}}
of
\infty
–categories which is the identity on
objects and sends a left adjoint functor to its right adjoint. Both
\mathcal{P\mkern-4.0mur}^{\textnormal{L}}
and

\mathcal{P\mkern-4.0mur}^{\textnormal{R}}
are complete and cocomplete and the homotopy limits in both
\mathcal{P\mkern-4.0mur}^{\textnormal{L}}
and

\mathcal{P\mkern-4.0mur}^{\textnormal{R}}
coincide with homotopy limits in the
\infty
–category of
\infty
–categories.

Definition A.1
.

A
smooth ind-scheme
over
S
is an object of
\Ind(\operatorname{\textnormal{Sm}}_{S})
, the
\infty
–category of
ind-objects in the category of smooth schemes over
S
with arbitrary
morphisms between them. A morphism of ind-schemes is smooth if it can be presented
as a colimit of smooth morphisms in
\operatorname{\textnormal{Sm}}_{S}
.

The goal of this section will be to generalize the definition of the stable
motivic homotopy category
\SH
to smooth ind-schemes over
S
. Our approach
is to use part of the six functor formalism for
\SH
, as established in

[
Ayo07a
,
Ayo07
]
for noetherian schemes and extended to arbitrary
schemes in
[
Hoy14
, Appendix C]
. An overview of the standard
functorialities, at least at the level of triangulated categories, can be found
in
[
CD09
]
.

by again composing with the equivalence
\mathcal{P\mkern-4.0mur}^{\textnormal{L}}\simeq(\mathcal{P\mkern-4.0mur}^{\textnormal{R}})^{\textnormal{op}}
.

More explicitly, if
(X_{i})_{i\in I}
is a filtered diagram of smooth schemes
over
S
and
X=\colim_{i}X_{i}
as an ind-scheme over
S
, then

\SH^{*}(X)=\holim_{i}\SH^{*}(X_{i})\quad\text{and}\quad\SH_{*}(X)=\hocolim_{i}\SH_{*}(X_{i}).

Note that
\SH^{*}(X)
and
\SH_{*}(X)
are equivalent
\infty
–categories since homotopy
limits along left adjoints in
\mathcal{P\mkern-4.0mur}^{\textnormal{L}}
correspond to homotopy colimits along
their right adjoints in
\mathcal{P\mkern-4.0mur}^{\textnormal{R}}
, see
[
Lur09
, section 5.5.3]
. This
description of
\SH(X)
also shows that it inherits the structure of a closed
symmetric monoidal, stable, presentable
\infty
–category, see
[
Lur12
, section 3.4.3,
Proposition 4.8.2.18]
.

f^{*}\dashv f_{*}
for a morphism

{

{\ignorespaces}{}{}
{}{}{}
\lxSVG@stroke}}

These data are functorial in

. In particular, if

,

over

determines an object

. If

is a smooth scheme over

, then

is
canonically equivalent to the

–suspension spectrum of

in

\SH(S)
; see
[
Ayo14
, Lemma C.2]
.

for the structure
morphisms. Suppose
Y\in\SH(S)
is arbitrary. Then we have natural
equivalences

\displaystyle\map_{\SH(S)}(\pi_{\#}\mathbf{1}_{X},Y)

\displaystyle\simeq\map_{\SH(X)}(\mathbf{1}_{X},\pi^{*}Y)

\displaystyle\simeq\holim_{i}\map_{\SH(X_{i})}(\mathbf{1}_{X_{i}},\pi_{i}^{*}Y)

\displaystyle\simeq\holim_{i}\map_{\SH(S)}(\pi_{i\#}\mathbf{1}_{X_{i}},Y)

\displaystyle\simeq\map_{\SH(S)}(\hocolim_{i}X_{i}/S,Y)

of mapping spaces. The Yoneda lemma implies that
X/S=\pi_{\#}\mathbf{1}_{X}\simeq\hocolim_{i}X_{i}/S
in
\SH(S)
.
∎
